# Findings ledger — the VISITED SET

> **Session handoff (local, gitignored, not in this directory):**
> `/home/joh/Documents/sin_and_punishment/sin-punishment-recompiled/HANDOFF-2026-08-18.md`
> It holds the perishable state — git position, which build is current, what is
> mid-flight — that this file deliberately does not.


**Read this file in full before expanding any node. It is deliberately short.**

`boot-debugging-2026-08-13.md` is a *journal* — chronological, 5,000+ lines,
expensive to search. This is the *index*: one line per fact, with a status. A
journal answers "what happened"; only this answers **"has this already been
checked?"**, which is the question that decides whether a node is worth
expanding.

Without it, the cheapest way to answer "do we know X?" is to re-derive X. That
is how the same ground gets covered twice — and a shortest-path search with no
visited set is not a shortest-path search.

## Where the evidence files live

The `evidence` column cites run logs by bare name (`a99-walk.log`, `bt.log`, …).
**Those are NOT in this repository.** From 2026-08-19 they are preserved on the
archive drive:

    /media/joh/extra/sin-punishment-archive/evidence/<date>/

**Everything cited before 2026-08-19 is GONE** — it lived in session-scoped
temp directories that no longer exist (T47). Treat those citations as naming
what *was* observed, not as something you can go and re-read. If a pre-08-19
claim matters, re-derive it rather than trusting an unreadable citation.

## Status vocabulary (revised 2026-08-18)

The old EST/OUT/WD tags hid *how* something was known. Roughly a dozen entries
were wrong this session, and they did **not** fail for lack of evidence — every
one cited real evidence, correctly gathered. They failed because the **claim was
broader than the evidence supporting it**. So the tag now records the kind of
support, because the kinds have very different reliability.

| tag | meaning | track record |
|---|---|---|
| **INTERVENED** | Changed one input, watched the symptom move. | **Never wrong yet.** Strongest available. |
| **MEASURED** | A tool reported this value. The claim is the *literal* reading, nothing more. | **Never wrong yet.** |
| **READ** | What the code says. Good for "what does this do"; **not** evidence about what actually runs. | Reliable when kept to behaviour-in-principle. |
| **NEGATIVE(scope)** | "Nothing does X." **The scope MUST appear in the claim itself.** | **4 of our failures.** The single most dangerous form. |
| **INFERRED** | Composed from other entries into a story. | **3 of our failures.** Never proof. |
| **WD** | Was believed, later overturned. Never deleted. | — |
| **OPEN** | Live question. | — |

## Rules for this file

1. **A claim is established only if a test ran that could have contradicted it
   and didn't.** A grep returning nothing usually could not have. A watchpoint
   held across the whole failure window could — but only with a positive control
   showing the same tool fires elsewhere.
2. **Every negative names its scope inside the claim.** Not "nothing calls this"
   but "nothing in splat's asm calls this". This rule alone would have prevented
   a third of our errors, including the day we declared a working probe broken.
3. **Load-bearing claims** — anything a fix or a decision will rest on — carry
   **Observed / Falsifier / Checked**. Everything else stays one line; full
   rigour on every entry is unaffordable.
4. **Distinguish the number from its meaning.** "CALLBACK=0" is MEASURED; "the
   list was empty" is INFERRED. Record them as separate claims or not at all.
5. An entry that gets overturned becomes **WD** — never deleted. The overturned
   belief is exactly what a future session would otherwise re-derive.
6. Add the entry when the finding lands, not at session end.
7. **Write it SHORT — the median entry is ~124 words and carries claim, status,
   evidence and falsifier fine.** `check_ledger.py` names every entry over 250
   words on each edit. That is a warning, not a gate: some entries earn their
   length. The point is that length becomes a decision instead of an accident,
   which is all it was on 2026-08-19 (**T51**).
8. **When a roll supersedes an entry's framing, COMPRESS the old text to one
   line — do not push it down under "Prior text follows".** Superseded framings
   must survive, because they stop resurrections (rule 5), but one line does
   that as well as a paragraph. Entries here are internally append-only by
   habit, and A99 had accumulated four full layers before anyone noticed.
   **This, not retained history, is what makes the file grow:** the
   2026-08-18 entries average 112 words, the 2026-08-19 entries 398 (T51).
9. **Archiving is the opposite discipline — be lazy about it.** It needs
   hindsight, since only later do you know what turned out to be load-bearing,
   and both archivable investigations are already done (T46).
   **Strict at write time, lazy at cut time.**
10. **Merging two entries that record the same lesson is allowed, and it is the
    only edit here that destroys structure rather than prose. So:**
    * **Never delete the merged-away ID.** It stays as a stub reading
      `MERGED into <ID>`, so every existing citation resolves and the visited
      set is preserved. `check_ledger.py` enforces that the target exists.
    * **Every instance keeps its own identity, fix and control** — one line
      each. "It happened five times" is the evidence; a merge that blurs the
      instances into one story throws away the reason to believe it.
    * **If the entries disagree, say which rule wins, in the class entry.**
      T20 and T22 both gave a run-count rule and T20's was superseded; a reader
      hitting T20 first was getting wrong guidance. **That, not size, is what
      merging is for** (T53).
    * **Do not merge on resemblance.** T16, T23 and T37 sit next to merged
      entries and were deliberately kept separate — see T53 for why, and do not
      re-merge them.

## THE USER QUEUE — work that needs the user's eyes, hands or ears

**Read by `scripts/user_queue.py`. It is a REMINDER, NEVER A GATE (T127) —
nothing here refuses a roll, because I cannot clear any of it myself and a
block only the user can lift would halt everything while they are away.**

**WHY A QUEUE AND NOT A NOTE PER ENTRY.** Every item here needs a real display,
a launch, and the user present. Run one at a time and the setup cost is paid
over and over; batched, one sitting clears several. **The queue exists to make
the sweep worth doing, not to make the items easier to find.**

**THE FAILURE THIS MUST NOT BECOME, and it has a precedent: T122.** Two
confirmed problems were written up as findings and never marked open, so the
router offered one candidate for five consecutive rolls while they sat there.
**A queue nothing forces you to empty is a way of feeling like you dealt with
something.** The alarm below is the load-bearing half; the list is the easy half.

**HOW ITEMS MUST BE WRITTEN — this is not style.** An item is an INSTRUCTION TO
A PERSON AT A KEYBOARD, plus what each outcome would mean, decided in advance.
A245 is the evidence: "at ~draw call 190 something happens to the life bar, and
scanning back and forth gives indeterminate results" — a real observation that
settled nothing, because what to look for was not pinned down first. **And I
cannot record these sessions: real mode records nothing by design (T59), so the
user's written answer IS the evidence.** An item I have to re-interpret
afterwards has failed.

**HOW OBSERVATIONS COME BACK (2026-08-21).** For anything with a recording, the
user annotates it in ELAN (`/home/joh/opt/ELAN_7.1/bin/ELAN_7.1`, see the
playbook) and exports tab-delimited text: **each note carries its own start and
end time**, so a described fault lands on exact frames instead of on a
conversation about which moment was meant. Live items at the keyboard — the F1
ones — still come back as written answers, because **real mode records nothing
by design (T59)**.

**CROSS-REFERENCE IS BIDIRECTIONAL AND CHECKED.** `SERVES` names the entries an
item would advance; `user_queue.py --check` fails on an ID that does not exist.
**An item is not SWEPT when the observation is made — it is SWEPT when an entry
records the result**, which is why a swept item must name that entry. Otherwise
this becomes A227: the data gathered and nothing resolved.

### The alarm

**`user_queue.py` reports three counts, and NONE of them needs a judgement:**

* **DEPTH** — live items. At 3+ a sitting clears more than it costs to set up.
* **AGE** — days since the oldest live item was queued. At 2+ it is starving.
* **BLOCKED** — distinct ledger entries the live items cite.

**BLOCKED is the mechanised form of "the last few checkpoints all pointed at
F1", and the reason it is counted this way rather than by inspecting
checkpoints: deciding whether a checkpoint "pointed at F1" is a judgement, and
`route.py`'s own history says a checker built on that kind of threshold trains
skimming rather than attention — T118 measured a 6-of-7 noise rate, and T122
recorded exactly this class as debt rather than mechanise it badly.** An entry
counts here only because an item explicitly names it, so the count is exact.

**The thresholds are arguable and are therefore printed, not hidden** — 3 items,
2 days, 3 entries. **The alarm DOES NOT FORGET (T120): there is no high-water
mark, so it keeps firing every run until the queue is actually swept.** A
reminder that reports itself once is a reminder you can lose.

## Load-bearing claims — full treatment

These are the claims decisions rest on. Each states what was *literally*
observed, what would falsify it, and whether that was checked.

### L1 — The START crash was a truncated symbol (fix applied)
- **Claim (MEASURED + INTERVENED):** `ovlfile02_func_800E4F34` was declared
  `size = 0x14`; the real function is `0x8C`. The generated C stopped after the
  first call, dropping the registration of the renderer's per-frame reset.
- **Observed:** generated C for that address contained 5 instructions ending at
  a `jal`; m2c of the same address emits 35 statements. splat's own `endlabel`
  and the next symbol both put the end at `0x800E4FC0`.
- **Falsifier:** the generated C containing the later statements, or splat
  agreeing with `0x14`, or the crash surviving the size correction.
- **Checked:** yes, all three. After the fix the missing registrations appear in
  a probe log and a 2h36m user-confirmed run never crashed (was 100% reliable).

### L2 — The attract freeze and the START stall are ONE bug
- **Claim (INTERVENED):** both are thread 4's stack overflowing onto the
  dispatch table at `0x8007AF0C`.
- **Observed:** a watchpoint caught `boot_func_800489C0` — a 4x4 matrix multiply
  that stages 16 floats on its own stack frame — writing the float `0.0463` over
  the function pointer. With `SNP_STACK_RELOC=4` graphics run through the START
  transition to 848 tasks at +30/s; without it the identical run stalls at ~360.
- **Falsifier:** the workaround failing to change the START symptom, or the
  clobbering write coming from somewhere other than thread 4's stack.
- **Checked:** yes. This is an intervention, which is why it is trusted.

### L3 — Thread 4's dispatch slots are NULLed at the stall
- **Claim (MEASURED):** `D_8007AF0C` and `D_8007AF10` hold valid addresses for
  12 samples, then read `0x00000000` for every sample to end of run.
- **Observed:** exactly that, via `SNP_WATCH` on both words.
- **Falsifier:** either slot holding a valid address after the stall.
- **Checked:** yes. **But note:** `func_80026598` legitimately calls
  `func_8004D500(0)`, so a NULL is not *by itself* proof of corruption — the
  corruption evidence is L2's float write, not this.

### L4 — The scene-graph data does not change during the failure
- **Claim (MEASURED, negative with a control):** neither the cycle node's child
  array (`0x8010273C`) nor the pointer to it (`0x8010278C`) is written between
  t=35s and t=62s, spanning the entire failure.
- **Observed:** two hardware watchpoints, zero hits.
- **Falsifier:** either watchpoint firing.
- **Checked:** yes — and the **positive control** is that the same tool fired
  immediately in its two other uses this session. Without that control these
  would be worthless non-results.

### L5 — The recursion genuinely gets deeper
- **Claim (MEASURED):** thread 4's stack low-water falls from `0x8007B748` to
  `0x8007AFE8` — 1,880 extra bytes — landing 220 bytes above the word L2 shows
  being clobbered.
- **Observed:** `low_sp` is an observed minimum stack pointer, not a tally, so
  it cannot be inflated by a miscounting loop.
- **Falsifier:** low_sp staying flat while the level counter rose (which would
  indict the counter instead).
- **Checked:** yes — the two moved together, so the depth growth is real even
  though the **`cycles=81` attribution remains unverified** (that detector has
  been wrong once already, see I2).

### L6 — splat's asm is not the whole ROM
- **Claim (MEASURED):** splat covers `0x80020000-0x8005FFFF` and `0x800E0000+`
  only; `0x80060000-0x800DFFFF` (620 functions) is absent — 5,853 `glabel`s vs
  6,827 recompiled functions.
- **Observed:** per-64KB histogram of `glabel` addresses, plus a symbol count.
- **Falsifier:** finding any `glabel` in the missing range.
- **Checked:** yes. **This is the rule-2 lesson:** two entries asserting
  "nothing in the ROM does X" were really "nothing in splat's asm does X", and
  both were wrong. Use `RecompiledFuncs/` for any completeness claim.

### L7 — The attract freeze was a truncated symbol (fix applied, user-confirmed)
- **Claim (MEASURED + INTERVENED):** `ovlfile20_func_800E5634` was declared
  `size = 0x54`; the real function is `0x118`. The generated C stopped before the
  epilogue, so the function allocated `0x18` of stack per call and never restored
  it — once per frame, until the stack walked onto the dispatch table at
  `0x8007AF0C`.
- **Observed:** generated C with `addiu $sp, $sp, -0x18` and **no restore and no
  `jr $ra`**, while control demonstrably returned to the dispatch loop. Corrected
  to `0x118`, the C gains `+0x18` at `0x800E5740` and `jr $ra` at `0x800E5744`,
  ending exactly on the next symbol `0x800E574C`.
- **Falsifier:** the drift surviving the size correction, or `fmax` still
  descending in the fixed build, or the freeze still occurring at gfx 1240.
- **Checked:** yes, all three, 3 isolated runs per arm. Baseline `fmax` descends
  `0x8073EA90 -> 0x8073CC30/0x8073CC78`; fixed `fmax` is flat across all three.
  On the real 8KB stack with no `SNP_STACK_RELOC`: gfx `2651 / 2649 / 2651` at
  `+30/s`, against a freeze at 1240. **User-confirmed on screen 2026-08-18:** the
  attract demo ran to the END of the video (t=146s, gfx 4349) with no input,
  where it previously froze at t~41s.
- **Note:** second instance of L1's class (BC-2). Both were found by the same
  signature — generated C with no epilogue. That is a sweepable pattern.

### L8 — The VI thread dereferences a null `ViState::mode` when the game autostarts (upstream PR 1)
- **Claim (INTERVENED):** with the `next_mode == nullptr` guard reverted, the VI
  thread SIGSEGVs within 3s of launch; with it applied, the VI thread never
  faults. This is a bug in **upstream** `N64ModernRuntime`, not in our port.
- **Observed:** 3 isolated runs per arm, the arms differing **only** by the
  10-line patch. The build is **bit-reproducible**, so each arm was confirmed by
  SHA-256 rather than assumed: reverted `6c44917297bcc06c…`, patched
  `21a6e7251e4284af…`, and the reverted rebuild reproduced the previous
  session's reverted binary byte-for-byte. **Reverted:** SIGSEGV at **2s / 2s /
  3s**, `VERDICT=CRASHED`, 15-line logs ending at font/asset init with **zero**
  game output. gdb on that build: `Thread 54 "VI Thread" … vi_thread_func()`,
  `si_addr = 0x1c`, faulting instruction `mov 0x1c(%r15),%edx` with `%r15` = 0;
  `0x1c` is `OSViMode::comRegs.hStart` (3 bytes padding + `type`, `ctrl` at 4).
  **Patched:** 3 runs, VI thread never faults, `+30/s` sustained to t=156.
- **Falsifier:** the reverted arm surviving its first VI iterations; the patched
  arm faulting in `vi_thread_func()`; or the reverted crash turning out to be an
  artifact of our own instrumentation.
- **Checked:** yes, all three. A 4th reverted run with `SNP_HEARTBEAT` **unset**
  also died at 3s, excluding our probe threads as the timing cause. The probe
  patch adds 724 lines to `events.cpp`, but all of it sits **after**
  `osViGetCurrentFramebuffer`; `update_vi` / `vi_thread_func` / `set_dummy_vi`
  are byte-identical to upstream apart from the fix.
- **CONTROL DESIGN — the trap this nearly fell into:** **both arms crash.** The
  patched arm still dies at ~158s on the unrelated A99 title-screen walker bug.
  A crash/no-crash reading of this experiment concludes the patch does nothing.
  The control only works because it discriminates on **time and faulting
  thread** — 2-3s in `vi_thread_func` vs 158s in the scene walker on thread 3.
- **Mechanism, READ against upstream `589bbf0` (0 behind), not inferred:**
  `ViState::mode` has no initializer while its sibling `retrace_count = 1` does,
  and `events_context` is static, so `mode` is a deterministic `nullptr`.
  `set_dummy_vi()` — the only thing that populates it pre-`osViSetMode` — is
  called **only** inside `if (!is_game_started())`, while `update_vi()`
  dereferences unconditionally a few lines later. `is_game_started()` is
  `game_status.load() != None` and `recomp::start_game()` stores `Running`
  directly, with no dependency on the game's MIPS code reaching `osViSetMode`.
  `update_vi()` ends `*get_next_state() = *get_cur_state()`, so **one** pre-start
  `set_dummy_vi()` populates both slots permanently — hence a first-iteration
  race only, which `SP_AUTOSTART=1` makes near-certain.

## Main findings — the attract freeze (CLOSED), then everything since

**This section's name was "Attract-mode freeze (gfx task 1240)" until 2026-08-19, and by then it was misleading.** The attract freeze is **SOLVED and user-confirmed** (L7: `ovlfile20_func_800E5634` declared `size = 0x54` against a real `0x118`). But the section kept absorbing everything that came after it, so it now also holds the **live title-screen crash frontier** (A99, A103, A110-A113) and **every tooling and method entry from T34 on** — 81% of the file, measured in T50. **A reader following the "read this file IN FULL" instruction was meeting the current frontier filed under a solved problem.** Renamed rather than split: splitting is the larger question T50 leaves open, and a wrong split is harder to undo than a stale name.

> **35 rows from the CLOSED attract-freeze / stack-drift investigation were moved to [`findings-archive-attract-freeze.md`](findings-archive-attract-freeze.md) on 2026-08-19** (T32). Nothing withdrawn, open, I-series, T-series, or still cited by live work was moved. `check_ledger.py` reads the archive, so citations of those IDs still resolve.


| # | status | finding | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| A307 | MEASURED (G_MTX parameter bytes recorded raw; 1 run of 200 s, 19 sampled frames, VERDICT=CLEAN; census self-test 18/18 unchanged) — **NOTHING EVER PUSHES A MATRIX. Only TWO parameter bytes occur in the whole run, `0x03` and `0x07`, and both appear in the attract and the tutorial alike. That explains `G_POPMTX = 0` completely — the modelview stack is never used, so it cannot overflow. MY OWN HYPOTHESIS FOR THIS CHECKPOINT IS DEAD. And the tutorial does NOT use more matrices than the attract: 128-129 against an attract range of 11-136** | **Roll #179, EXPLOIT (drew 0.326, eps 0.3), WITNESS `0962fa`, target A218, taking A302's own named next step.** **THE HYPOTHESIS, stated before the measurement so its death is legible: A302 measured `G_MTX` 45 (attract) against 128 (tutorial) with **`G_POPMTX` = 0 in BOTH**. F3DEX2's modelview stack is 10 deep. If a meaningful share of 128 matrix ops pushed without popping it would overflow, and everything after would draw under a garbage transform — which is precisely the 'drawn off-screen' branch A302 left open.** **MEASURED AND IT DOES NOT HAPPEN: across 19 frames spanning attract, start screen and tutorial the parameter byte is **only ever `0x03` or `0x07`**. Under F3DEX2's encoding — where the push flag is stored INVERTED (`p ^ G_MTX_PUSH`), so bit0 SET means NOPUSH — those are MODELVIEW/LOAD/NOPUSH and PROJECTION/LOAD/NOPUSH. **Every matrix operation replaces; none stacks.** No pushes means no pops needed, no overflow, and no accumulated transform drift.** **THE DECODE-INDEPENDENT HALF, and it is the part that does not rest on a convention I did not measure: **only two distinct parameter bytes occur anywhere in the run, and both scenes use both.** Whatever `0x03` and `0x07` mean, the working scene and the broken one are using the SAME transform structure. That holds even if the inversion is the other way round.** **RAW BYTES WERE RECORDED RATHER THAN AN INTERPRETATION, deliberately: I have hand-derived a packing wrongly twice today (T141's scissor, T146's fixture). A histogram of raw bytes is checkable by anyone; a decoded 'PUSH: 47' would look authoritative whether or not it was right, and the decode can now be disputed separately from the counts.** **A CORRECTION TO AN IMPLICATION OF A293, which compared two single frames: the tutorial does not do more matrix work. Tutorial frames sit at **128-129**; attract frames range **11 to 136**, with task 2400 exceeding every tutorial frame sampled. **Matrix COUNT does not distinguish the scenes at all**, and A293's 45-vs-128 was a property of the two frames it chose.** **WHAT REMAINS OF 'DRAWN OFF-SCREEN', scoped honestly: the matrix CONTENTS. Every load replaces the current matrix, so a bad one affects only the draws until the next load, and 126 of the 128 are modelview loads. Reading the actual numbers means following the segmented pointer to a 4x4 fixed-point block — the same shape as A302's viewport decode, and not done here.** **ONE RUN IS ENOUGH: the claim is that a parameter byte takes only two values across 19 frames of one run and that both scenes use both — the attract frames are the control for the tutorial frames, in the same run, and a second run cannot introduce a third value into this one.** **SO WHAT: the transform stack the game uses turns out never to be stacked at all — every step replaces the last — so the guess that it was quietly overflowing and throwing the scenery somewhere invisible is wrong, and the scene that works and the scene that does not are doing the identical kind of transform work.** **Falsifier: any frame using a `G_MTX` parameter byte other than `0x03` or `0x07`, or any `G_POPMTX` at all** | 2026-08-21 (roll #179, witness `0962fa`); `evidence/2026-08-21/mtx.log`, 19 frames |

A224 scanned this window and found 100% dark. **Those are in tension and nobody had looked at the frames.**** **MEASURED, `signalstats` YMAX per frame: **16, 16, 16 ... 16 for 7.20 s**, then 242 at 7.47 s, 243, 245, 253. YMAX=16 is video black, so the maximum pixel ANYWHERE in the frame is at the floor. **Not a faint logo, not a logo in a corner — nothing.**** **THE ALIGNMENT IS DERIVED AND CHECKABLE, not assumed: the heartbeat gives task 9 at t=1 s and task 159 at t=6 s, so the brackets (tasks ~10 and ~160) fall at heartbeat t≈1.0 s and t≈6.0 s. Content begins at task 170 ≈ 6.4 s by the same mapping and appears in the video at 7.25-7.47 s, giving a recorder-to-game offset of ≈0.9-1.1 s. **The brackets therefore land at video t≈1.9 s and t≈6.9 s, both deep inside the all-black stretch, and a 0.5 s bracket cannot hide between 4 Hz samples.**** **SO THE TENSION RESOLVES AGAINST THE DRAW: the quad is submitted, textured, palette-loaded — and contributes nothing. A291's 'the artwork is loaded every frame and never drawn' understated it; the frames that DO draw it also show nothing.** **AND THE BORDER IS INVISIBLE FOR A REASON WE ALREADY MEASURED: A301 found all four border rects run with `color=UNSET`, no `G_SETFILLCOLOR` anywhere in these frames. A fill with a leftover register value that happens to be black is a black border on a black screen — consistent with YMAX=16 rather than a separate puzzle.** **SCOPE, AND IT MATTERS HERE: this is what the HEADLESS RECORDING shows. A287's unresolved aside is that the user sees geometry warping on a real display that does not appear in recordings, so 'the recording shows black' is not identical to 'the screen shows black'. **This is the third independent instrument to say dark over this window (A224, A222's reference comparison, and now peak luma), but all three read recordings.**** **ONE RUN IS ENOUGH: the census and the pixels come from the SAME run, which is the design — comparing a submitted list against a different run's frames is the misalignment this avoids — and A224 measured the same darkness on a different run at 0.5 s resolution.** **SO WHAT: during the seconds when the opening artwork should be on screen the picture is not merely missing the logo, it is completely black in every single frame, including the handful that do hand over a textured shape to draw.** **Falsifier: any frame between t=0 and t=7.2 s of this recording with a peak luma above 16** | 2026-08-21 (roll #178, witness `146d22`); `evidence/2026-08-21/run_game-195338.mp4` against `logo-operands.log` |

That is evidence the rule is insufficient, not that I was careless once.** **WHAT IS ACTUALLY OPEN, unchanged by this checkpoint: the consumer blocks at its loop head forever; the producer stops calling send; A294 established the message is never sent rather than lost. **Nothing here touches WHY the producer stops**, which remains the whole question.** **ONE RUN IS ENOUGH: the claim is that ten backtraces in one run are identical to each other, which a second run cannot alter, and the substantive content is A228/A240's, both already carrying their own evidence.** **SO WHAT: nothing moved forward — I confirmed at the last frame something already measured in the middle and settled by a debugger six rolls ago, because I read the list of related findings twice without opening any of them.** **Falsifier: a backtrace at the boundary differing from the ones A228 recorded mid-run** | 2026-08-21 (roll #177, witness `94129a`); `evidence/2026-08-21/stall-bt.log`, `stall-dense.log` |

Do not generalise this to the tutorial."** That warning is now cashed. **MEASURED across 200 s: tasks 300/600/900/1200/2400/4200 issue BOTH a colour and a depth clear; tasks 3000, 4800, 5100 and 5700 issue the DEPTH CLEAR ONLY.** 9 colour-buffer full clears against 25 depth-buffer ones.** **SO A219'S MECHANISM IS CONFIRMED AT THE OPERAND LEVEL: nothing wipes the picture in the tutorial, so whatever is drawn persists, and only things that MOVE leave a trail — which is exactly the static-clean/animated-smeared split the user reported and A219 has always recorded.** **AND A297'S HYPOTHESIS IS RESURRECTED. That entry proposed the colour buffer might never be cleared, named the missing operand, refused to claim it, and A300 appeared to kill it. **A300 killed it for early attract frames only.** The hypothesis was right about the tutorial and I retracted it too broadly.** **WHY AN UNCLEARED FRAME IS FINE IN THE ATTRACT AND NOT THE TUTORIAL, stated as reasoning rather than measurement: task 3000 is an attract frame that also skips the colour clear and renders correctly — because a full-screen video overwrites every pixel, so there is nothing to leave behind. RSS climbs at a constant 16-24 KB/s straight across the boundary and threads stay at 58.** **WHAT THIS ELIMINATES, and it is a family rather than a single story: the list DEGENERATING before the stop, a TRUNCATED or malformed final list, a gradual WIND-DOWN of graphics work, and the game's task loop dying — the audio branch of the very same loop runs on indefinitely. **A211 said 'sudden arrest at full health'; this is that, measured at the level of the last thing submitted.**** **A SECOND RUN NOW CARRIES T144's RESULT: that entry measured RSS and threads flat across the stall on one run and I flagged it as such. This is an independent 215 s run reproducing it — constant slope, no step, threads unchanged. **The resource-exhaustion explanation is now refuted twice.**** **SO A211 NARROWS TO A BRANCH, NOT A BREAKDOWN: everything the producer emits is well-formed right up to the last frame, and then one specific submission path stops being taken while its sibling continues. **What is NOT established is why that branch stops** — this measures the output, not the decision, and the decision is CPU-side where A283 and A268 have already been looking.** **ONE RUN IS ENOUGH: the claim is about the SHAPE of the last lists in a run that reached the ceiling, and the 6,000 lists before them in the same run are their control. | A302 | MEASURED (viewport decode added to the census, 2 controls VERIFIED TO FAIL; 1 run of 200 s, 19 sampled frames spanning attract, start screen and tutorial, VERDICT=CLEAN) — **THE VIEWPORT IS CORRECT AND IDENTICAL EVERYWHERE. Every sampled frame in the whole run reads the SAME structure at the SAME address with the SAME values — scale 640,480,511 / trans 640,480,511, which derives to x 0..320, y 0..240, i.e. exactly the full 320x240 screen. A218'S 'DRAWN OFF-SCREEN' BRANCH IS DEAD AT THE VIEWPORT** | **Roll #174, EXPLOIT (drew 0.448, eps 0.3), WITNESS `c79e4f`, target A218.** **WHY THE VIEWPORT AND WHY NOW: A218 was down to two candidates — drawn off-screen, or drawn behind something — after A296 killed the orphan target, A297 killed the scissor rectangle and the overwrite, and A300 confirmed the colour buffer IS cleared. The viewport is the operand that decides where transformed geometry lands, and **nothing had ever read it**. A293 measured `G_MOVEMEM` at 3 in BOTH scenes, but that is a COUNT — and counts are not operands, which is the exact trap A297 had to close for scissors.** **MEASURED, 19 frames from task 300 to 5700: `nvp=1, unresolved=0` in every one; **one distinct viewport in the entire run**, at one address `0x802C3B20`. Attract frames that render correctly, the start screen, and the tutorial frames missing their scenery ALL read the same full-screen rectangle. Tutorial frames carry n=611 triangles, so geometry is submitted (A263/A293) and the viewport it is submitted under is right.** **THE DECODE HAS THREE WAYS TO BE WRONG AND THE CONTROL COVERS EACH: halfword ORDER within a word, SIGNEDNESS (a negative translate must not read as ~65000), and following the SEGMENTED pointer at all. **Verified to FAIL at 14/16** — swapping the halfword order and defaulting an unresolvable pointer each broke exactly the two viewport controls and nothing else. The second control matters most: an unresolvable pointer is COUNTED, never defaulted, because an invented viewport would be indistinguishable from a real one and would answer A218 with fiction.** **SCOPE, AND IT IS THE HONEST LIMIT OF THIS RESULT: the viewport maps normalised coordinates to the screen; it is NOT the only transform. Geometry can still land off-screen through a bad projection or modelview matrix, and `G_MTX` is 45 in the attract against **128** in the tutorial (A293) — unread. **So 'drawn off-screen' is not eliminated; the VIEWPORT branch of it is**, and it was the cheapest and most likely one.** **ONE RUN IS ENOUGH: the claim is that a value is IDENTICAL across scenes inside one run — the attract frames are the control for the tutorial frames, in the same list, and a second run cannot make two frames of this one differ. A237 measured two independent runs byte-identical in command counts across the opening.** **SO WHAT: the window the game draws its picture into is set to the whole screen and is exactly the same in the scene that works and the scene that does not, so the missing scenery is not being pushed off the edge of the display by that setting — which leaves the transforms, which nobody has looked at yet.** **Falsifier: any frame whose viewport differs from scale 640,480,511 / trans 640,480,511** | 2026-08-21 (roll #174, witness `c79e4f`); `evidence/2026-08-21/viewport.log`, 19 frames; `selftest-vp.log` 16/16, `selftest-vp-broken.log` 14/16 |
| A301 | MEASURED (logo window re-captured with the operand probe A297/A300 added; 1 run of 20 s, 114 frames sampled at 10-frame resolution, VERDICT=CLEAN) — **THE LOGO WINDOW NEVER SETS UP A FRAME AT ALL. All 16 frames from task 10 to 160 use ONE render target, issue NO full-area clear, and NEVER set a fill colour. At task 170 all of it appears at once — three targets, two clears, fill colours, 346 triangles. AND A ZERO-AREA SCISSOR `0,0-0,0` OCCURS IN ALL 16 LOGO FRAMES AND IN NONE OF THE OTHER 98** | **Roll #173, EXPLORE (drew 0.210, eps 0.3), WITNESS `1ceb18`, target A225 at p=0.25.** **WHY A NEW RUN: `logo-dense.log` predates the operand fields. Only tasks 10 and 160 carry a triangle (n=1). **Task 170 onward: `ncimg=3, nrect=6`, TWO full-area clears, 6 colour-set rects, n=346 triangles.** The change is simultaneous across all five properties.** **SO A225 IS REFRAMED, and this is the point of the checkpoint: the question was "why is the logo not drawn". **These frames do not fail to draw a set-up frame — they never set one up.** No clear, no fill colour, a single target. A298 showed the geometry commands are absent; this shows the frame scaffolding is absent with them.** **TRIPLE BUFFERING STARTS AT 170, AND THE ARITHMETIC SETTLES IT: `0x8038F800`, `0x803B5000`, `0x803DA800` are spaced **exactly 153,600 bytes = 320 x 240 x 2**. Three 16-bit colour buffers. During the logo window the game cycles these one per frame with `ncimg=1`; from 170 it names three per frame. `0x803DA800` is the same colour target the attract and tutorial use (A300), and `0x80000400` — the depth buffer — appears only from 170.** **THE ZERO-AREA SCISSOR IS THE FIRST THING EXCLUSIVE TO THE BROKEN WINDOW, and every other candidate so far has failed exactly this test: `0x80000400` (A290), `0xFFFCFFFC` (A297) and the letterbox border (A296) all turned up in scenes that render correctly. **This one does not: 16 of 16 logo frames, 0 of 98 others.**** **AND I AM NOT CLAIMING IT CAUSES ANYTHING — THE MECHANISM DOES NOT WORK. It sits at `seq=20` and a normal scissor is restored at `seq=25`, while the bracket frames draw their triangle at `seq=45`. **By the time anything is drawn the scissor is `12,8-308,231` again.** It cannot clip a draw that happens 20 commands after it is undone, and it cannot suppress a clear that is never issued (`nrect=4`, no full-area fill). **A perfect correlate with no working mechanism is a lead, not a finding** — and the striking part is only that `seq=20` is exactly where the working scenes put their colour clear (A300).** **ONE RUN IS ENOUGH: the claim is that a property is ABSENT across 16 frames and PRESENT from a sharp boundary in the same run — the post-170 frames are the control for the pre-170 ones, in the same list, at the same sampling interval. A237 measured two independent runs byte-identical across this exact window and A270 reproduced the 15/136/15 structure on a different run.** **SO WHAT: during the seconds when the opening artwork should be on screen the game is not drawing a picture badly, it is not preparing a picture at all — no wiping, no colour set, one surface instead of three — and everything switches on together the instant the next scene starts.** **Falsifier: a logo-window frame issuing a full-area fill, setting a fill colour, or naming more than one render target** | 2026-08-21 (roll #173, witness `1ceb18`); `evidence/2026-08-21/logo-operands.log`, 114 frames |
| A300 | MEASURED (probe extended with the render target IN FORCE at each rect, 1 new control VERIFIED TO FAIL; 1 run of 25 s, VERDICT=CLEAN) — **`0x80000400` IS THE DEPTH BUFFER, AND THERE ARE TWO LARGE FILLS, NOT ONE. Every sampled early frame clears COLOUR at `seq=20` (`0x00010001`, into `0x803DA800`) and DEPTH at `seq=25` (`0xFFFCFFFC`, into `0x80000400`). A297'S HYPOTHESIS — that the colour buffer is never cleared — IS FALSE, killed one checkpoint later by the exact measurement A297 named as the way to kill it** | **Roll #172, EXPLORE (drew 0.072, eps 0.3), WITNESS `723896`, target A219 at p=0.27.** **THE ONE LINE A297 NAMED, WRITTEN: `G_SETCIMG` now carries a sequence index and every rect records the target IN FORCE when it executes. **15th control added and VERIFIED TO FAIL** (T71 gate 2) — broken by recording the frame's FIRST target instead of the running one, which scored 13/14 with exactly that control failing and the other thirteen unaffected. The fixture switches target BETWEEN two rects precisely so "first", "last" and "in force" cannot agree.** **MEASURED, sampled every 60 tasks across 25 s: `fill 12,8-307,231 seq=20 color=0x00010001 into=0x803DA800` then `fill 12,8-307,231 seq=25 color=0xFFFCFFFC into=0x80000400`, repeating in every sampled frame. Across all rects: 56 into `0x803DA800`, 9 into `0x80000400`.** **SO A257'S ANOMALY IS FULLY DISCHARGED, and it took four entries: A257 flagged `0x80000400` as "the first kilobyte of RDRAM, where no framebuffer belongs" and refused to call it a finding; A290 showed it appears in the working scene too and left "our decode is wrong" and "this value is normal" BOTH open; A296 showed no orphan target exists; **this closes it — the decode was right and the value is normal, because it is not a colour framebuffer at all. A region containing several functions needs several exits — each ends in a return or a tail-call jump. **There is one return and no jumps.**** **AND THE DECLARED BOUNDARY IS DEMONSTRABLY WRONG, visible in two adjacent words: offset 0x3C is `8C830018` (`lw $v1, 0x18($a0)`) and 0x40 is `ACA30004` (`sw $v1, 0x4($a1)`). **The declaration cuts between a load and the store that consumes its result.** No function ends there.** **THIS OVERTURNS A149's REFUTATION OF A96, AND ONLY BY MEASUREMENT (T107): A149 is MEASURED and its factual core stands — 20 of 23 of our declarations agree with splat at 0x40, three do not. **What falls is the INFERENCE built on it.** A149 read the region as "many blocks, not one function" from a count of **88 `alabel`s** — but an `alabel` is an ADDRESS label, a branch target inside a function, not a function start (A281 saw three of them mid-function in splat's own asm). **88 branch targets in 1,240 bytes is an ordinary function with loops; it is not 88 functions, and 88 functions would need 88 exits.**** **AND THE AGREEMENT A149 RESTED ON IS WORTH LESS THAN IT LOOKED, which A259 and A281 already established: splat's 0x40 is AUTO-DETECTED, not known. **Twenty tools agreeing with each other is one measurement repeated when they share a method.** A262 was opened precisely to get a control "not borrowed from a tool we have found to be guessing" — this is that control.** **SO A96's ROLL-#39 CLAIM IS VINDICATED FOR OVLFILE07: it said "one alloc, one restore, one `jr $ra` at `0x800E4C90`, gap `0x4D8` matching exactly". **`0x800E4780 + 0x510 = 0x800E4C90`. IT PAID OUT IMMEDIATELY — A211's "sudden arrest at full health rather than resource exhaustion" was INFERRED FROM THE FRAME-RATE SHAPE and is now MEASURED: memory rises at an identical rate on both sides of the stall, with no discontinuity at all** | **THE GPU FIX WAS NOT INSTRUMENTATION, IT WAS A DISCARDED BUFFER. RT64 already prints `Device Name`, `Device Vendor` and `Driver Version` to **stdout** at startup, and `run_game.sh` has always merged both streams — yet the lines were in **2 of 34** logs. stderr is unbuffered; stdout to a FILE is block-buffered, and every run ends in `kill -9`, which throws a partial buffer away. **We were writing it and then destroying it, which looks exactly like never writing it.** `stdbuf -oL` on the binary: 0 → 3 lines, verified before and after.** **WHY IT MATTERS: A287's unresolved aside is that warping appears on the real display and not in our recordings. Returns `?` rather than 0 when /proc is unreadable; an invented zero reads as a process using no memory.** **THE RESULT, 1 run to 215 s, VERDICT=STALLED: RSS **468,672k → 501,780k** (+33 MB over 215 s) and threads **flat at 58** throughout. Across the stall boundary — gfx tasks go +30, then +13 at t=207, then **+0 forever** — RSS keeps climbing at **16-28 KB/s per second, identical before and after**, with no step, no plateau and no acceleration. `other` (audio) tasks hold **+30/s the whole way**, so the task loop never stops.** **SO A211'S PREMISE IS CONFIRMED BY MEASUREMENT RATHER THAN BY THE SHAPE OF A CURVE: nothing is exhausted at the stall. That entry reasoned it from "no decay, no leak-shaped curve" in the frame rate; this reads the resources directly and agrees.** **WHAT I DELIBERATELY DID NOT DO, and it is the interesting half: the queue counters `g_viprobe_*` are what the frontier actually wants standing, and they are gated behind `SNP_VI_PROBE` because they increment INSIDE the message-queue send/recv path. **Making them always-on means instrumenting the hot path where the stall itself lives.** A probe that changes what it measures is worse than no probe, so they stay opt-in.** **ONE RUN IS ENOUGH: the claim is CONTINUITY ACROSS A BOUNDARY INSIDE ONE RUN — the pre-stall segment is the control for the post-stall segment, which is the design and not a concession (as in A293/A296) — and a second run cannot make a discontinuity appear in this one.** **SO WHAT: every run now records which graphics card and driver produced it, and the standing health numbers show the game is not running out of anything when it stops drawing — memory climbs at exactly the same gentle rate before and after, which we had believed but never actually checked.** **Falsifier: a run whose memory or thread count changes slope at the stall** | 2026-08-21 (user-directed, no roll consumed); `evidence/2026-08-21/telemetry.log`, `gpulog-before.log` vs `gpulog-after.log`, `test_display_isolate.py` 17/17, census self-test 14/14 |

Post-fix that costs ~5 ms. **Naming it so it is not rediscovered as a defect.**** **THE REST IS CLEAN: an AST sweep of all 41 scripts for `subprocess.run`/file reads inside loops returned 2 — one `ffprobe` per sampled frame, one guard invocation per test case. Both inherent; neither a defect.** **SCOPE, INSIDE THE CLAIM: this profiled the tools that run every checkpoint. **The one-off analysis scripts were not measured**, and nothing here says they are efficient.** **SO WHAT: the check that runs every single time I touch the record got a fifth faster, because one helper was reading another file off the disk seventeen hundred times a run — and the cause I had assumed turned out to account for almost none of it, which I found out only by measuring instead of trusting the guess.** **Falsifier: any control that passed before this change and fails after, or a re-timing on an idle machine that does not reproduce the 1.27 -> 1.01 s gap** | 2026-08-21 (user-directed, no roll consumed); 5-run timings, `cProfile` before and after, all self-checks re-run |
| T136 | INTERVENED | **USER-DIRECTED: ~5 MINUTES OVER ANY TIME BUDGET IS FINE — and writing the control for it caught T100's pattern AGAIN, in the control itself.** **THE RULE, the user's standing decision: the deadline bounds when new work may START, not work already in flight. A checkpoint truncated to land on the minute — no entry, no `SO WHAT:` line, no commit — costs the NEXT session more than the overrun saves. It is slack, not a budget: not cumulative, and it does not license starting a checkpoint already known not to fit.** **WIRED IN TWO PLACES FROM ONE DEFINITION (T121): `session.py` gained `GRACE`, and both `status` and `end` read it, so the tool and the skill cannot drift into disagreeing about the rule. Inside the grace `status` says finish-what-is-in-flight; past it, it still shouts.** **THE CONTROL FAILED TO DISCRIMINATE ON ITS FIRST WRITE, AND THE VERIFY-TO-FAIL STEP IS THE ONLY REASON I KNOW: the fixture set the deadline to `time.time() - (GRACE + 60)` — **computing its own needle from the constant under test**. Setting `GRACE` to `10**9` — a grace that removes the deadline entirely — still scored **16/16**. The needle moved with the thing it was measuring.** **FIXED BY ASSEMBLING THE NEEDLE FROM PARTS: the offsets are now literals (1 minute over, 11 minutes over), so the policy number lives in the control where the implementation cannot move it. Re-verified: **15/16 with `GRACE` unbounded, exactly that control failing**, and the failure message prints the absurd grace it was granted.** **THIS IS ANOTHER INSTANCE OF T100 — "a control that greps its own file is the usual way one stops discriminating" — and it is worth counting because the shape was NEW to me: not a control reading the file it checks, but a control **deriving its threshold from the constant it checks**. Same failure, different disguise, and the pass looked perfect.** **SO WHAT: running a few minutes past a timer is now explicitly allowed instead of being a judgement call I made silently each time — and the check that keeps that allowance from quietly becoming unlimited only works because I tried to break it and found it did not.** | 2026-08-21 (user-directed, no roll consumed); `scripts/session.py` 16/16, verified to FAIL at 15/16 with `GRACE` unbounded; `.claude/skills/timed-session/SKILL.md` |

The value is stashed BEFORE the `jal` at `0x3240C` and jumped to after, which is an ordinary tail-return. **One of the two open indirect edges is not an edge to anywhere new**, and treating it as a dispatch would have sent the next checkpoint hunting a table that does not exist.** **`jr $r1` at `0x323B0` IS a dispatch, and the five instructions before it are unambiguous: `lw $26, 0x0($29)` loads a command word; `srl $1, $26, 7` takes its top bits; `andi $1, $1, 0xFE` masks to an EVEN index 0-254; `lh $1, 0x0($1)` loads a 16-BIT TARGET from a table at low address; `jr $1`. **Halfword targets and a byte-indexed table is exactly how RSP microcode dispatches commands** — IMEM addresses fit in 16 bits.** **WHY THIS IS THE STEP A253 ASKED FOR: A253 measured ZERO direct edges from the audio body into the DPC region, against 23 edges within it, with a decoder control finding 55 branches landing in the body. Its result stands. **What it could not exclude was these two indirect jumps — and one is now excluded outright, while the other has a FINITE, ENUMERABLE target set** rather than being unfollowable in principle.** **NOT ESTABLISHED, AND IT IS NOW THE WHOLE QUESTION: the table's CONTENTS. The index is masked to 0-254, so the table is at most 128 entries in the first 255 bytes of the ucode's data. **Reading it enumerates every command this dispatcher can reach, which would settle A253's remaining scope completely — reachable means someone must decide what an RDP kick does in a renderer with no RDP; unreachable means stub it and A97 collapses.** Not read here: the table lives in DMEM at run time and its ROM provenance was not established in this checkpoint, and guessing an offset is how T49's confident wrong table happened.** **SO WHAT: of the two places the audio code could have jumped to somewhere unknown, one turns out to be an ordinary return and the other picks its destination from a small list — so instead of an open-ended question there is now a short list to read.** **Falsifier: an instruction between `0x323F0` and `0x32414` that overwrites `$5`** | 2026-08-21 (roll #151, witness `c2e81f`); ROM decode at `0x32388-0x32418` |

A LARGE value exits immediately, which also matches the function's own design pattern: every existing case returns the value meaning 'the thing you are waiting for has already happened'.** **SO A97 IS RE-COSTED UPWARD IN KIND, NOT IN SIZE: the reads are still trivial. **The writes are the work**, and until someone decides what a DPC kick should do in a renderer that has no RDP, the microcode cannot be recompiled correctly however many cases are added.** **NOT ESTABLISHED: whether the DPC writes are on a path this game's audio task actually executes, or dead code inside a shared blob. **That single question decides whether this is a design problem or a stub-it-out problem**, and it is the cheapest thing to ask next.** **NEXT: determine whether the DPC write sites are reachable on the audio task's live path — a static reachability read from the ucode entry, no run needed. | T125 | INTERVENED — **the stale-binary trap is closed at the point of USE rather than by making every build slower. A runner now says so when the binary it is about to launch is older than the sources it was built from — and the test is a FACT, not a threshold** | **User-directed: "is there a way we can automate all the builds being updated to avoid that trap - or would that be a bad idea?"** **IT WOULD HAVE BEEN A BAD IDEA, AND SAYING SO WAS THE USEFUL PART. Always building both trees costs minutes on EVERY build, including the many that only feed a 25-second smoke test. A permanent tax to prevent an occasional mistake gets worked around — someone adds a fast path and the trap returns. So the check went where the staleness MATTERS: the moment a binary is about to run.** **THE TEST IS THRESHOLD-FREE, which is why it is defensible: it does NOT compare the two binaries to each other — "how much drift between them is acceptable" is a judgement nobody can defend. It asks whether THIS binary is older than any source it was built from, which is a fact. That also catches the MIRROR case (rebuild debug, leave release stale) that a compare-the-pair rule would not notice at all.** **IT WARNS, IT DOES NOT REFUSE, and that is deliberate: running an older binary on purpose is frequent here — every A/B against a build snapshot is exactly that. (2) **Same defect class found in `build.sh`**, which had `*) shift ;;` — a typo like `--lable X` would have built and snapshotted with **no label** and said nothing, and `--no-recomp-typo` would have silently run a full recompile. (3) **`--help` added to all 35 scripts** — shell scripts print their own header block (self-maintaining, no duplicated usage text), Python scripts print `__doc__`, and the three argparse scripts were left to argparse, which gives better help. `audit.py` also gained strict rejection since it writes `docs/audit-log.md`. | I17 | MEASURED + FIXED | **NOW CAUGHT AUTOMATICALLY — `lint_hooks.py` refuses this class before the build.** The check reads every `MEM_*` call in a hook body and errors if an operand is narrowed to `unsigned`, or if **neither** operand sign-extends (no `ctx->rN`, no nested `MEM_*`). **ERROR, not warning**, because of how I17 failed: it did not crash visibly, it produced a clean plausible FALSE result. **Writing the check surfaced a fact worth keeping: `MEM_*` arguments are COMMUTATIVE and N64Recomp emits both orders** — `MEM_W(0X18, ctx->r29)` for `sw $ra,0x18($sp)` but `MEM_BU(ctx->r20, 0X0)` for `lbu $a0,0x0($s4)` — because the macro computes `(reg) + (offset)`. My first version assumed the register was always the second argument and flagged the second form, i.e. **valid generated code**. Caught by a control case, not by inspection, which is the I-series pattern exactly. **Controlled 10/10, wired as `scripts/test_lint_hooks.py`**, including the **verbatim** offending hook body from 2026-08-19 as a permanent regression case, and including the nested `MEM_W(0, MEM_W(8, ctx->r4))` form as a must-NOT-flag case — over-flagging would get the whole channel ignored (T29). Tests run on temporary copies and assert `lint_hooks.py` itself is byte-unchanged. Original entry follows. **Does NOT rest on A105 — A105's conclusion is withdrawn, and this stands regardless: it is about the instrument, not the hypothesis.** **Instrument defect (14th), mine, TWO in one hook — and the first one produced a convincing FALSE RESULT.** Writing the A105 zeroing hook — A105's conclusion is withdrawn, but this entry is about the instrument I built, not about what it concluded: **(a)** I zeroed `arg1` bytes, but the allocator allocates `(arg1 + 0x10) & 0xFFFF` — the **mask** means a large `arg1` yields a much smaller block, so the loop ran off the end. **(b)** far worse, I passed the address to `MEM_W` as a 32-bit `unsigned int`. The macro is `*(int32_t*)(rdram + (((reg) + (offset)) - 0xFFFFFFFF80000000))`, so **`reg` must be the SIGN-EXTENDED int64_t register**; a 32-bit `0x8007xxxx` promotes to `0x000000008007xxxx`, the subtraction wraps, and the write lands at a wild host address. **Why this mattered:** both bugs made the game SIGSEGV at 3s with the arm ON and 158s with it OFF — a large, clean, entirely spurious difference that reads exactly like "zeroing the allocation breaks the game". Had I stopped there I would have recorded a strong result that was purely my own bug. It was caught only by asking *where* it faulted: gdb put `#0` inside `boot_func_80026960` itself — my hook's function — not in game code. **Rule, alongside "byte access to RDRAM needs `^3`": `MEM_W`/`MEM_B`/`MEM_H` take the sign-extended `ctx->rN`, never a narrowed copy. Put the displacement in the OFFSET argument (`MEM_W(i + 0x10, ctx->r2)`), never in the base.** **And the general one, third time today: when an intervention produces a big clean difference, check the instrument before believing it** — a broken probe fails loudly and looks like a finding | 2026-08-19 |

The old lossy-source path took 4.9 s on a comparable run, so lossless capture costs ~1.5 s despite a 20x larger source.** **THE GENERAL SHAPE: all four gaps were caught by asking 'is X actually there?' and none by reading what I had written.** Prose describing a discipline is not the discipline | 2026-08-19 (user-prompted) |

A degenerate hash would collapse these and the check would fail. **PRESENCE vs ABSENCE is built into the output.** The per-frame BEST DISTANCE is always printed, so a near miss is visible instead of being swallowed by the threshold; and `--fps 0` examines EVERY frame, which is what makes an absence claim possible at all. **Do not pass a coarse `--fps` and then write 'never' — that is A93/A161 with extra steps.** **TWO SCOPE LIMITS, both real.** (1) **References must come from OUR OWN build, not from the ares captures.** ares renders at roughly 240p with the N64's video-interface filtering/AA emulated; RT64 does not match it pixel-for-pixel. **The ares set remains authoritative for SEQUENCE and scene IDENTITY — it is why we know the title screen is the green logo card — and is NOT usable for pixel matching.** (2) **References are tied to the current renderer settings and window size.** Change resolution or filtering and they must be rebuilt; the self-check's discrimination numbers are the canary. **A moving cinematic needs many references or it reads as unmatched** — attract currently matches only sporadically, and the tool says `(unmatched)` rather than guessing, which is the correct behaviour | 2026-08-19 (user-prompted) |
| T87 | INTERVENED — **recordings are auto-cropped to the game window, and the cropper REFUSES rather than guessing. 46 MB of recordings became 14 MB with a proof that nothing was discarded** | **T83's recordings are 1280x720 with the game a 640x480 window centred in them (Xvfb has no window manager), so most of every frame is black padding.** **THE OBVIOUS TOOL IS THE WRONG ONE.** `ffmpeg`'s `cropdetect` finds the bounding box of non-black CONTENT, and the game's own image has black borders inside its 640x480: a title-screen frame measures **591x425 at 344,136**, well inside the window. **Cropping to that would have sliced off real game pixels and looked perfectly fine** — the failure mode this project keeps paying for. | A158 | **REFUTED IN PART by A174 (roll #95): its FIX — move `text_offset` past the boot stub — cannot work, because the body does not follow the stub in the same blob; the stub DMAs the body from RDRAM over IMEM 0x080 and jumps into what it wrote. Its READING of librecomp's contract STANDS, and its 'IMEM 0x1080 is not a plausible entry' observation is CONFIRMED and now explained.** Was: READ (librecomp's `run_task` + RSPRecomp's config struct + `rsp/audio.toml`; zero runs) — **A155's defect is OURS, not librecomp's: our toml starts the recompiled ucode at the BOOT STUB, and librecomp's contract is that it starts at the task BODY with DMEM already primed. librecomp is correct to have no IMEM DMA path — under its contract no ucode should ever run a boot stub** | **A155 found the audio ucode DMAing its own text to `SP_MEM_ADDR = 0x1080` (IMEM) and librecomp routing it to DMEM. This entry answers the question A155 left open — whether that is a librecomp gap to work around — and the answer is no.** **THE CONTRACT, read off `recomp::rsp::run_task` (`librecomp/src/rsp.cpp:36-50`). librecomp performs the RSP boot ITSELF, on the host:** `memcpy(&dmem[0xFC0], task, sizeof(OSTask))` — the OSTask copy the ucode reads at `0xFC0`; `dma_rdram_to_dmem(rdram, 0x0000, task->t.ucode_data, 0xF80 - 1)` — **`ucode_data` into DMEM 0, i.e. the command handler jump table**; then `ucode_func(rdram, task->t.ucode)`. **Both of the things the ROM's boot stub does at `0x10A4` and `0x1008` are already done before the recompiled function is entered.** RSPRecomp confirms the intent from the other side: it emits the parameter as `[[maybe_unused]] uint32_t ucode_addr` and never reads it, because under this contract the body needs no self-load. **WHAT OUR TOML DOES INSTEAD.** `rsp/audio.toml` sets `text_offset = 0x32280` / `text_address = 0x04001000`, so `n_aspMain` begins at **IMEM `0x000` — the boot stub** — and re-runs boot code librecomp has already emulated: the redundant (harmless) `ucode_data` re-load at `0x10A4`, then **the IMEM self-load at `0x1008` that librecomp cannot perform, then `jr $a3` to IMEM `0x080`.** **So the assert and the DMEM overwrite A155 measured are downstream of a configuration error in this repo.** **AND RSPRecomp SUPPORTS THIS PROPERLY — we simply do not use it.** `RSPRecompilerConfig` carries `overlay_slots` (`RSPRecompilerOverlaySlotConfig{text_address, overlays[]}`), and the generator emits an `imemToSlot` map plus a per-slot `offsetToOverlay` map. **`rsp/audio.toml` declares no overlay slots at all.** **THE FIX, INFERRED AND NOT VERIFIED (T57):** move `text_offset`/`text_address` past the boot stub so the function begins at the body. The flat layout is already consistent — the body loads to IMEM `0x080` and RSPRecomp labels IMEM offset `i` as `L_(0x1000+i)` — **so only the ENTRY is wrong, not the addressing**; and `0x1000`-`0x107F`, the IMEM DMA included, would then not be part of the recompiled text. **RSPRecomp has NO entry-point field separate from `text_address`, so this is the only lever.** **WHAT BLOCKS APPLYING IT, and it is the next bounded check:** the body's start is not established. If the stub were exactly `0x80` bytes the body would be ROM `0x32300`, but IMEM `0x1080` statically disassembles as `andi $v0, $v0, 0x100` — the middle of a DPC-status test, not a plausible entry. One of them is, and this is it.** **The boot path is unconditional and was traced instruction-for-instruction:** `0x1000` `j 0x1064` -> `0x1064` tests `task->flags & 2` (yielded) -> `0x108C` loads `task->ucode_data`/`_size` -> **`0x10A4` DMAs `ucode_data` to `SP_MEM_ADDR = 0`, i.e. DMEM 0 — that is the handler jump table** -> `0x10B0` DMA wait -> `0x10BC` `jal 0x103C` -> `0x10C4` `j 0x1008` -> **`0x1008` loads `task->ucode` (OSTask `+0x10`) and DMAs it to `SP_MEM_ADDR = 0x1080`** -> `0x1034` `jr $a3` to `0x1080`. **`SP_MEM_ADDR` bit 12 is the IMEM/DMEM select, and `0x1080` has it SET: this is the ucode self-loading its text to IMEM `0x080` and jumping into it.** **librecomp does not implement that.** `DO_DMA_READ` is `#define`d unconditionally to `dma_rdram_to_dmem` (`rsp.hpp:94`); there is no IMEM target anywhere in the header. | A124 | READ — static, cross-validated against the ROM; **answers A99's open question: what sets the walker's `$s0`** | **The walk carries THREE PARALLEL ARRAYS, all indexed by one child byte, and NONE of them is bounds-checked.** At the recursive call (`0x800339C8`-`0x800339EC`): `idx = $a0 & 0xFF` from the child list, then `$a0 = $s2 + idx*0x14` (node array), `$a1 = $s1 + idx*0x20` (matrix array), and **`$a2 = $s0 + idx*4`** — and `$a2` becomes the callee's `$s0` (prologue `addu $s0, $a2`). **So `$s0` is the base of a stride-4 array that is advanced by `idx*4` at every level of the descent.** **Independent confirmation of A110 falls out:** the node stride computed here is `idx*5*4 = idx*0x14`, exactly the stride A110 read out of the scene data and A107 measured live. Fixed by resolving the geometry once, with its default. **Method note: every one of the three isolation defects today was found by RUNNING the thing and reading its own output, none by reading the code.** **`docs/install-wishlist.md` added** at the user's request: things worth installing, each with the incident that motivated it and the cost it would have avoided. | T59 | INTERVENED in part — **the gdb wrappers ran on the user's real display with no input protection, and Xephyr never solved the window problem anyway** | **USER-RAISED, 2026-08-19, and both halves were things I had wrong.** **(a) `gdb_watch.sh` and `gdb_fault.sh` had NO isolation at all.** They set `SDL_VIDEODRIVER=x11` and inherit the real `DISPLAY`, unlike `run_game.sh`, which has started a nested Xephyr since it was written. **Four runs today — one fault dump and two watchpoints, each 3-4 minutes — put a live game window on the user's desktop with the keyboard connected to it.** T23 measured a single injected `A` press turning a healthy run into a SIGSEGV, and these runs had no `input_events` check either. `gdb_watch.sh` predates me; **`gdb_fault.sh` is mine and copied its launch line without checking, twenty minutes after I had been using the isolated runner.** FIXED: both now start Xephyr on `:7` with the same `SNP_VISIBLE=1` override, and tear it down on exit. **(b) MY CORRECTION, from the user: Xephyr windows have ALWAYS reached the display.** I wrote that the fix meant "no window should reach your display". **That is wrong and it misdescribes what Xephyr is** — a NESTED X server, which opens a window on the real display and serves `:7` inside it. So `run_game.sh` has been showing a window all along; what Xephyr actually buys is **input** isolation, since keystrokes only reach the game when that window has focus. Worth having, and not what I claimed. **RESOLVED by T60: `Xvfb` was installed and wired in, so runs are headless by default and no window appears at all.** Original text follows. **Truly headless needs `Xvfb`, which was then absent and needed apt/sudo** — the project already recorded that in `minimize_window.py`, whose whole existence is the workaround. **`boot_screen_check.sh` and `freeze_check.sh` iconify their window; `run_game.sh` and the gdb wrappers do not.** Extending that to Xephyr's own window is plausible but UNVALIDATED: minimising the host server could throttle or stop rendering inside it and perturb every measurement, and the 2026-08-14 note that "the game keeps rendering while minimized" was measured on the game window directly, not on a nested server. **Do not enable it without an A/B on gfx rate.** **Result check: A123 was re-run under the new isolation and is unchanged** — same address, same `0x02006300` -> `0x02000000`, same writer, same call chain, three runs total | 2026-08-19 |

Print everything" — and the code said **`bt -1`**. In gdb a NEGATIVE count prints the **outermost** n frames, so every run since printed `clone3` and nothing else. **The fix that was meant to remove truncation truncated harder, and the comment asserted the opposite of the behaviour.** Caught when the A99 writer was finally trapped and the backtrace was empty of anything useful. Fixed to a bare `bt`; the very next run gave the full chain, which turned out to be the finding (A123). **(b) Ranged `disassemble` is no safer than `x/i`.** A102 was once misled by `x/i $pc-N` decoding mid-instruction; I re-made that mistake in `gdb_fault.sh`, "fixed" it by switching to `disassemble $pc-32,+48` — **and got `add %al,(%rax)` for the first three lines anyway, because `$pc-32` is still an arbitrary offset.** Both scripts now use a bare `disassemble`, which decodes the whole current function from its own entry point. **Verbose beats wrong.** **Both defects share a shape worth naming: the tool kept working and kept printing, so nothing looked broken. A truncated backtrace and a misdecoded instruction are indistinguishable from a real result unless you already know what you expect to see** | 2026-08-19 |
| A122 | MEASURED (gdb, debug build, 1 run) — **the faulting address is in the HEAP, not in overlay data: A113/A114's chain does not describe the fault** | **`$s0 = 0x8013C278`, `*(0x8013C278) = 0x02000000`, and dereferencing that is the SIGSEGV.** Read directly off the crash with `scripts/gdb_fault.sh` against `build-debug`, so `ctx` resolves and no derivation of `k` was needed. **Mechanism, from the aligned disassembly:** `rax = *(ctx->r16)` -> `test rax,rax` / `je` (the game's own zero guard, which passes because the value is NON-zero) -> `rdx = 0x80000000 + rax` -> `movslq 0x0(%rbp,%rdx,1)` with `%rbp` = rdram base, i.e. a read at **rdram + 0x82000000**, far outside the 8MB. Registers at the fault: `rax=0x2000000`, `rdx=0x82000000`. That reproduces A102's account exactly, now with the source address as well as the value. **`0x8013C278` IS `obj + 8`** — A105 (withdrawn as to conclusion; its logged address is an observation) recorded the allocation base as `0x8013C270` and A106 measured `obj=8013C270`, so this is the third independent sighting of that object. **WHAT THIS REFUTES.** A113 traced `$s0 = $a2 = desc->unk4` and A114 followed that into overlay data at `0x802C____`. **That trace is correct for the OUTERMOST call and irrelevant to the fault:** the backtrace shows the crash at **recursion depth 5** (`funcs_4.c:271`, under four frames at `funcs_4.c:665`), and the recursive call reassigns `$s0`. The generated C passes ONE shared `ctx`, so `ctx->r16` at the fault is the innermost invocation's, not the outermost's. **This is T57's lesson biting immediately, in my own work:** every component of the A113/A114 trace was verified; the composing step — that the `$s0` at the fault is the `$s0` the outer call set up — was never checked, and it is false. **A107 could not have caught it either: its probe was gated to nodes in `0x802E____`, so a heap node was invisible to it by construction.** **NEXT, and it is now a known address rather than a derived one:** `scripts/gdb_watch.sh 0x8013C278` names whoever writes `0x02000000` there. Every field the callback touches (`unk84`, `unk88`, `unk94`, `unkE0`, `unkE8`) fits inside 0xF8. **(2) The allocator does not zero:** `func_80026960` allocates `size + 0x10`, writes **only the 0x10 header** (`unk0=0`, `unk4=callback`, `unk8`/`unkC` list links), links it, and returns `base + 0x10`. There is **no memset and no clear of the 0xF8 body**. **(3) The constructor does not fill the gap:** `func_800B18B0` assigns 14 fields — `unk5 unk7 unk8 unkB unkC unkE unk28 unk2C unk30 unk34 unk7C unkDC unkF0` and the header byte at `-0xF` — and **assigns neither `unk84` nor `unk88`**, which are exactly the two fields A99's walker call reads as its scene root (`var_s0 = arg0->unk88` or `arg0->unk84`). **`unk10` is also never assigned**, and it gates the walk (`if (!(arg0->unk10 & 1))`). **So the scene root is stale heap contents — which is precisely why it is NON-ZERO garbage that passes the game's own `beq $v0, $zero` guard (A102), and why the values look like uninitialised memory (A103).** **Claim strength: this is READ (what three functions do) plus INFERRED (composed into a story) — NOT established.** It does not yet explain why the real console does not crash; the candidates are that something else writes `unk84`/`unk88` before the first walk and is missing or late here, or that the heap happens to hold benign values on hardware. **DECISIVE NEXT EXPERIMENT, and it is an intervention rather than another guard (A103's whole point):** zero the allocated body — **via a scratch hook on `func_80026960`, NEVER by editing `RecompiledFuncs/`** — and re-run. If the crash disappears or moves to the game's own zero-check path, uninitialised memory is confirmed as the mechanism; if it survives unchanged, this story is wrong and something is actively writing garbage. Either result is worth the build | 2026-08-19; m2c on `func_800B18B0` / `func_80026960` |
| A104 | INTERVENED | **[Re-verified 2026-08-19 ~11:00 on the post-T45 binary `79718f89dcdb8865`, since the binary changed after the original measurement: 2,138,112 samples, 0 non-zero, peak 0/32767, VERDICT=CLEAN. The silence is unchanged and is NOT an artifact of the older generated C.]** **A97 advanced: the SIG0 hack is what silences the audio, and BOTH possible constants are wrong.** Baseline re-measured on today's build (`21a6e725…`, `VERDICT=CLEAN`, 40s): **2,162,688 samples, ZERO non-zero, peak 0/32767** — A97's premise survives the symbol fixes that reached the menu. **The ucode is not missing and is not failing:** `get_rsp_microcode` (`src/main/main.cpp:68`) handles **only** `M_AUDTASK` and prints `Unknown RSP task type` on anything else, and `rsp.cpp` prints `No registered RSP ucode` / `exited unexpectedly`; **none of those three appear** in the run. Scope: one 40s CLEAN autostart run's stderr — and the channel is positively controlled, since `[audio] device open` reaches the same log by the same `fprintf(stderr)`. So every non-gfx task was audio, ~30/s, dispatched and exiting normally. **Mechanism, READ from `rsp/audio.cpp:70`:** the hack forces `r8 = 128` where the ucode does `mfc0 $8, SP_STATUS` / `andi $8,$8,0x80` / `bne $8,$zero,L_1050`. Non-zero always takes the branch, and `L_1050` sets `SP_STATUS = 0x5200` and executes `break 0` -> `RspExitReason::Broke`. **So the hack drives the audio ucode down its ABORT path on every single task** — and `Broke` is the *expected* exit reason, which is exactly why nothing complains. The fall-through (`r8 = 0`) is `jr $ra`, a normal return that continues processing. **INTERVENED (3/3):** setting `r8 = 0` and rebuilding gives `gfx_tasks=1 other=1` and then a **permanent stall** — one audio task enters the ucode and never returns, blocking the SP task thread so nothing further is processed. All three runs `VERDICT=DEGRADED`, which is the point: the degradation IS the result, and no build-to-build audio comparison was drawn from them (T22). Restoring gives `21a6e725…` byte-for-byte and a CLEAN `+30/s` run. **Conclusion: `expected_c0_reg_value(SP_STATUS)` cannot be a constant — `0x80` buys silence, `0x00` buys deadlock.** The real fix is modelling SP_STATUS/SIG0, which is what the (now closed) upstream SIG0 item was about. **A97 stays OPEN**; this names the mechanism, it does not fix it. **Confound disclosed and eliminated:** the first no-SIG0 build also carried 127 stub probes injected by T39, so its arms differed by two things; the 3 runs above are the clean re-run, arms differing only by `rsp/audio.cpp:70` | 2026-08-19 |

## START crash — **SOLVED 2026-08-18** (was: NULL deref in `boot_func_8003860C`)

**The answer, in four lines.** `symbols/sinpunishment.syms.toml` declared
`ovlfile02_func_800E4F34` as `size = 0x14`; the real function is `0x8C`. The
generated C therefore stopped after its first call, silently dropping the
`func_800B4EE0(0, 0)` that registers the renderer's per-frame reset. Scene 2 then
reset nothing, the list counts stayed stale from scene 20, the unconditional
rewind `ptr -= count*4` drove the pointers below their buffers, and the sort
dereferenced NULL. **Fix: `size = 0x8C`.** User-confirmed: no crash in 2h36m.

### Load-bearing — reuse these


**ARCHIVED 2026-08-19:** 26 supporting rows of this closed investigation moved to [`findings-archive-start-crash.md`](findings-archive-start-crash.md) — B4, B5, B9, B10, B14-B17, B19, B24, B25, B27, B32, B34, B39, B40, B45, B52, B55, B57, B58, B60-B63, B66. They are still cited normally; `check_ledger.py` reads that file. Everything WITHDRAWN, every I/T row, every OPEN row and every row a staying entry depends on remained here.

| # | status | finding | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|

| B20 | EST | **ARCHITECTURE.** `func_80025E44` (thread 3) is a **scene loop, not a frame loop**: load scene -> `func_80026288()` **once** -> register per-frame callback `func_800261FC` -> spin until `D_80068A95` changes -> repeat. Thread 4 does all per-frame work. Hence only 3-4 driver calls per run | 2026-08-18; m2c |

| B38 | EST | `scripts/overlay_map.py` computes every asset chunk's unpack address offline (base `0x802A0370`, 27 chunks, 23 scenes) with a working Yay0 decompressor | 2026-08-18 |
